THE CREATIVE INDEPENDENT — INTERVIEW ANALYSIS
Built from the _posts collection: 2,132 published interviews, ~2,095 distinct interviewees.
(23 unpublished/scheduled posts in _posts were excluded.)

==============================================================================
HEADLINE ANSWERS
==============================================================================

  Communing with nature ................. 393   (307 in recs lists + 86 more who advocate it in conversation)
  Staying hydrated ...................... 36   (25 in recs lists + 11 more in conversation)

  Maggie Nelson's Bluets ................ 6

  Working parents ....................... 242   (176 of them talk about balancing kids and work)

  Working day jobs ...................... 109 now   (281 have held one at some point)
  "Late" to their creative practice ..... 30

  Creative block ........................ Change what you're doing or where (46 of 265)
  Sitting with discomfort ............... Accept not-knowing, trust the process (35 of 165)
  Thinking outside entrenched systems ... Build your own institutions (30 of 174)

==============================================================================
HOW THIS WAS DONE — AND WHAT THE NUMBERS MEAN
==============================================================================

  Source: every interview in _posts — 2,132 published interviews with ~2,095
  distinct people. 23 posts marked published: false (a test post, old drafts,
  and interviews scheduled for Sep–Oct 2026) are excluded. Counts are of
  distinct people: someone interviewed twice counts once, and in joint
  interviews the specific person is named.
  Three kinds of evidence were used. (1) Each interview's "recommends" list,
  read in full. (2) TCI's own vocation and tag fields. (3) The interviews
  themselves: answers were narrowed by keyword to relevant passages, then
  classified against a written rule for each question by Claude Sonnet agents,
  and a sample of every question was checked by hand (Claude Opus), with
  manual corrections where the rule was misapplied. The interviewer's
  questions never count as evidence, except where the interviewer states a
  plain fact about the interviewee ("you're also a father").
  Counts from recommendation lists and vocations are close to complete. Counts
  that come from conversation — parents, day jobs, late starters, hobbyists,
  and teachers beyond those whose vocation says so — are LOWER BOUNDS: someone
  who never mentions their kids in a way the search catches isn't counted.
  For the advice questions, the number beside each theme is how many
  interviewees gave that advice among those who talked about the topic. The
  ranking is the finding; the absolute numbers depend on how the passages were
  narrowed. A handful of advice-givers (at most three per topic) came from the
  23 unpublished posts; they're removed from the totals, so individual theme
  counts may be off by one or two.
  Full name lists with links are in the APPENDIX at the end.


==============================================================================
1. HOW MANY PEOPLE RECOMMEND…
==============================================================================


COMMUNING WITH NATURE — 393 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: a recommendation of time in nature or outdoors — walks or hikes in
  natural settings, forests, parks, beaches and swimming outdoors, gardening,
  birding, stargazing, a named natural place. Books or albums *about* nature
  don't count.
  307 put it in their recommends list; 86 more tell readers to get outside, or
  describe time in nature as something they rely on, in the interview itself.
  Another 66 recommend plain "walking" with no natural setting — not counted
  above. It is one of the most common kinds of recommendation on the site.
    · Pattie Gonia: "If you feel dead inside, get the fuck outside"
    · Meredith Monk: "Go outside and be open to whatever you see and hear."
    · Soccer Mommy: "Dyer Observatory. I love going to their telescope nights."
    · Ian Svenonius: "Get off the internet when you can. Go outside."
    · Brooke Van Poppelen: "I've gotten back into communing with nature, which is something that I've missed."

STAYING HYDRATED — 36 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: recommends drinking water or staying hydrated. 25 in their recommends
  list, 11 more in conversation. Another 9 recommend a specific water or
  seltzer (Topo Chico, La Croix, Mela watermelon water…) without the hydration
  framing — not counted above.
    · Anjimile Chithambo: "Drink water. If you're thirsty, you're already dehydrated."
    · Chelsea Ryoko Wong: "Talk to strangers. Dine solo. Drink water out of a wine glass."
    · Tara Vancil: "Water. Drinking it. Floating, splashing, submerging yourself in it. Listening to it. Keeping your cats away from it."
    · Somi: "it's all about drinking water."

LUCID DREAMING — 0 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  No one recommends it, in a recommends list or in conversation. The only
  mention in the corpus is the interviewer describing Chloe Benjamin's novel
  The Anatomy of Dreams, about researchers who study lucid dreaming.
  The nearest practice — keeping a dream journal, or dreamwork — is
  recommended by 8 people:
  - Allegra Hyde  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/author-allegra-hyde-on-creative-work-as-a-space-for-new-possibilities/
      "Dream dictionaries. It's important to pay attention to our subconscious, which is often smarter and more knowi"
  - Becca Harvey  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musicians-matt-berninger-and-becca-harvey-on-how-making-things-can-change-your-life/
      "Keep a dream journal"
  - Danielle Aykroyd  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-danielle-aykroyd-vera-sola-on-trusting-your-vision/
      "Keep a dream journal: It's crazy in there! In some cultures dream life is more important than waking life"
  - Delia Cai  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-delia-cai-on-finding-new-ways-to-manage-your-time/
      "Usually it's a few bullet points about what I dreamed about plus the activities of the day"
  - Emma Ruth Rundle  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-and-visual-artist-emma-ruth-rundle-on-showing-up-and-doing-the-work/
      "Spend 5 to 10 minutes of your morning writing down what you can remember about your dreams from the night befo"
  - Matt Marble  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/artist-author-and-media-producer-matt-marble-on-art-as-a-devotional-practice/
      "Dreamwork saved my life and helped me overcome drug addiction. I still personally return to this practice and"
  - Maya Binyam  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-maya-binyam-on-prioritizing-inspirational-relationships-over-professional-connections/
      "Keeping a dream journal. I find mine painfully boring to write and re-read, but it makes for better dreams."
  - Zackary Drucker  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/zackary-drucker-on-documenting-your-life-in-your-work/
      "Having conversations with loved ones in my dreams, then writing everything down"

THE ARTIST'S WAY (Julia Cameron) — 16 people recommend or endorse it
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  3 put it in their formal "recommends" list; 13 more endorse it in the interview
  itself ("I love The Artist's Way", "changed my musical journey", "I recommend
  [morning pages] to every artist"). The practice people actually take from it is
  morning pages — 11 of the 16 describe doing them.
  In the recommends list (3):
    - LP Giobbi — also: "changed my musical journey"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/producer-and-dj-lp-giobbi-on-not-waiting-around-for-inspiration/
    - Pat Regan — has done it twice; "it does help me"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/comedian-pat-regan-on-getting-to-know-your-own-inner-workings/
    - Katya Apekina — morning pages and artist dates
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/novelist-katya-apekina-on-how-family-informs-the-creative-process/
  Endorsed in the interview (13):
    - Neil Goldberg — "a cheesy self-help book that I find very helpful"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/neil-goldberg-on-process-and-performance/
    - Somi — "I really believe in it"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/vocalist-and-songwriter-somi-on-trusting-your-own-voice-and-making-your-own-model/
    - Kiki Slugqueen — morning pages
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/drag-queen-and-intuitive-reader-kiki-slugqueen-on-listening-as-performance/
    - Jess Viscius — artist dates
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-and-visual-artist-jess-viscius-on-staying-true-to-yourself-across-all-your-creative-outlets/
    - Emma Ruth Rundle — "I love The Artist's Way"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-and-visual-artist-emma-ruth-rundle-on-showing-up-and-doing-the-work/
    - Jordan Kisner — morning handwriting, "right out of The Artist's Way"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-jordan-kisner-on-following-your-curiosity/
    - Bethany Cosentino — morning pages
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-bethany-cosentino-on-silencing-your-inner-critic/
    - Jennifer Lewis — did the pages "for like five years in a row"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-teacher-and-publisher-jennifer-lewis-on-giving-your-work-the-time-it-needs/
    - Cricket Arrison — "very helpful to me"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/filmmaker-and-performer-cricket-arrison-on-abandoning-five-year-plans/
    - Katie Gavin — does it "once every couple of years"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-katie-gavin-on-refusing-to-censor-yourself/
    - Tunde Adebimpe — morning pages since the late '90s
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-actor-and-visual-artist-tunde-adebimpe-on-having-a-master-plan/
    - Bells Larsen — first felt like an artist after completing it
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-bells-larsen-on-collaborating-with-your-past-and-future-selves/
    - Nastasya Popov — "I recommend them to every artist"
      https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-and-director-nastasya-popov-on-why-its-important-to-keep-making-things/
  Mixed or passing (not counted): Ada Lea ("cringey in many ways" but kept the
  morning pages), Shura (approving reference), H. Pruz (inspiration for a game).
  Excluded: Christina Catherine Martinez (joke at its expense in a rec for another
  book), Angela Autumn ("Artist's Way vibes"), Sara Levine (the interviewer's line),
  Julia Cameron (the author).

A LOCAL RESTAURANT — 138 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: names a specific independent restaurant, café, bakery, bar or food
  stall in their recommends list. Another 9 recommend only a chain (White
  Castle, Starbucks, Din Tai Fung…) — not counted above. The places named are
  listed in the appendix.
    · Joseph O. Legaspi: "Taco stall on the corner of Gleane St. and Roosevelt Ave. in Elmhurst, Queens"
    · Jenny Toomey & Kristin Thomson: "I love the Avalon Lounge in Catskill, NY... they serve excellent bibimbap bowls late into the evening"
    · Cecilia Dean: "Whit's End at the Riis Park Beach Bazaar on the beach in Rockaway (open all winter). The food is worth the trip."
    · Danez Smith (chain): "A number 1 with cheese from White Castle."


==============================================================================
2. HOW MANY PEOPLE ARE…
==============================================================================


WORKING PARENTS — 242 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: says they have children (any age, biological, adoptive or step). Since
  everyone interviewed has a working practice, every parent counts as a
  working parent. 176 of the 242 talk specifically about fitting parenting
  around their work — time, money, energy, how kids changed the practice.
  Lower bound.
    · Raquel Berríos: "going to work and coming back home and working on music after putting my daughter to bed"
    · Agathe Snow: "Thursday and Friday, fully devoted to the farm, and then Saturday is for [my son] Cyrus"
    · Patrick Leder: "I cannot stress how important having a child was to my career"
    · Matthew Rohrer: "being a parent is great, but it also sucks"

MAKING THINGS ACROSS MULTIPLE DISCIPLINES — 630 people (~30%)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: TCI's vocation line for them spans two or more distinct making
  disciplines — e.g. "Musician, Painter" counts; "Poet, Writer" or "Writer,
  Editor" doesn't. Roles like curator, editor, teacher or founder aren't
  counted as making disciplines. 470 span two disciplines, 127 span three, 33
  span four or more. Cross-check: 635 interviews carry editorial tags from two
  or more disciplines, so the two measures agree.
  Most common pairings:
    · Visual Art + Writing: 67
    · Music + Visual Art: 57
    · Music + Writing: 53
    · Performance + Writing: 39
    · Design + Visual Art: 34
    · Music + Performance: 30
    · Film/Video + Writing: 27
    · Film/Video + Music: 19

WORKING DAY JOBS — 109 people now; 281 have held one
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: holds a job outside their creative practice to support it — waiting
  tables, retail, office work, teaching done to pay the bills, and so on. A
  job that IS the career they're interviewed about doesn't count. 109 had one
  at the time of their interview; 167 had one earlier; the rest of the 281 had
  one at an unclear time. Lower bound.
    · Menno Pasveer: "I work one day a week and every holiday still, as a floor cleaner, to make my stable living."
    · Ephraim Asili: "Yes, I have a day job. I'm a college professor."
    · Sarah Beth Tomberlin: "I wrote the majority of At Weddings when I was working 40 to 45 hours a week at a Verizon store doing sales."
    · Josh Fadem: "I worked at an archival video store for 10 years."

"LATE" TO THEIR CREATIVE PRACTICE — 30 people
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: calls themselves a late bloomer or says they started late (17), began
  a core practice at 30 or older (11), or left a non-creative career for it
  (2). Lower bound — this depends on people mentioning an age or the word
  "late".
    · Gerald Busby: "why didn't I start writing music until I was 40 years old"
    · Sean Baker: "I started late—actually, I'm 47"
    · David Duchovny: "when I became an actor, I thought I was too old to start acting"
    · Brenda Ann Kenneally: "I first picked up a camera when I was 30"
    · Marisa Anderson: "The first solo guitar record I did came out when I was 40"


==============================================================================
3. WHAT'S THE MOST COMMON ADVICE FOR…
==============================================================================


CREATIVE BLOCK — 265 people gave advice on this
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Most common: change what you're doing, or where you're doing it — switch
  medium, project or room rather than staring at the same wall. The next two
  answers pull in opposite directions and are nearly tied: step away and trust
  it will come back, or push through on a schedule.

   1. Change what you're doing, or where you're doing it ............. 46
   2. Step away, rest, and trust the timing .......................... 40
   3. Push through with discipline and routine ....................... 35
   4. Feed the well — read, watch, look at other people's work ....... 33
   5. Diagnose the real problem (it's often not a "block") ........... 31
   6. Lower the stakes — make something deliberately bad ............. 23
   7. Let go of conscious control (chance, Oblique Strategies) ....... 23
   8. Move your body ................................................. 23
   9. Reframe the block as normal or temporary ....................... 22
  10. Protect your headspace from distraction and critics ............ 19
  11. Talk it out — get feedback or company .......................... 19
      Also: keep a bank of old ideas to return to (17), impose deadlines or
      constraints (15), mine your own life (15), just start (12).

    · Tegan Quin: "I'll put it on different speakers. I'll walk around or do
      something else... it's a change of scenery."
    · Rickie Lee Jones: "I just wait. I accept that I might not write
      anymore... maybe it's not the end of the world."
    · Henry Rollins: "I do try to write 1,000 words a day. It's just like
      going to the gym."
    · Kelsey McKinney: "you don't actually have writer's block, you're just
      not done reporting."
    · Laurie Anderson: "try doing your very worst work. Do the worst song you
      can possibly think of."

SITTING WITH DISCOMFORT — 165 people gave advice on this
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Most common: accept not-knowing as a normal — even valuable — part of making
  things, rather than a problem to solve before you start. Close behind: stay
  with the feeling instead of rushing to resolve it, and seek discomfort out
  on purpose.

   1. Accept not-knowing and trust the uncertain process ............. 35
   2. Sit with the feeling instead of rushing to resolve it .......... 29
   3. Deliberately leave your comfort zone ........................... 27
   4. Reframe fear with a specific mental move ....................... 23
   5. Put the vulnerable, embarrassing material into the work ........ 13
   6. Persist through hardship that's part of the job ................ 11
      Also: self-compassion (9), lean on others (8), treat discomfort as a
      signal (8), stop caring how you'll be judged (7), use deadlines to
      contain open-ended anxiety (5).

    · Eliza Callahan: "You have to have some outsized tolerance for not
      knowing."
    · Tania Pérez Córdova: "I try to celebrate not knowing."
    · Coco Mellors: "You have to be able to sit through the discomfort. And
      it's really, really hard."
    · Ash Goh Hua: "if it feels uncomfortable, it's a good thing and you
      should lean into it."
    · Orenda Fink: "When fear takes hold, I ask myself two questions: 'Is this
      true? And so what?'"

THINKING OUTSIDE ENTRENCHED SYSTEMS — 174 people gave advice on this
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Most common: build your own institution — the gallery, label, venue, school
  or system you couldn't get into. The top three are nearly tied and fit
  together: build your own thing (30), with other people (28), without waiting
  to be let in (27). Several push back on the lone-DIY version: "it's not DIY,
  it's DIT: do it together."

   1. Build your own institutions, spaces and infrastructure ......... 30
   2. Rely on community, collaboration and chosen family ............. 28
   3. Do it yourself — don't wait for permission ..................... 27
   4. Trust yourself and ignore outside judgment ..................... 24
   5. Protect the work from market pressure .......................... 21
   6. Confront, subvert or route around gatekeepers .................. 18
   7. Redefine success on your own terms ............................. 17
   8. Treat rest and slowness as resistance .......................... 14
   9. Build financial independence and resourcefulness ............... 13
      Also: redistribute resources and reject growth for its own sake (8),
      reach your audience directly (4).

    · Ian MacKaye: "We want our own system and you can't stop us from building
      it."
    · Constanza Valenzuela: "it's not DIY. It's DIT: do it together"
    · Eliot Glazer: "No one is going to do it for you, you have to do it
      yourself."
    · Matt Fantastic: "I would rather have a dope life than a bunch of money."
    · Hiba Ali: "I think not working is productive. I think not doing things
      is productive."

GETTING STARTED — 308 people gave advice on this
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  By a wide margin: just start — before you feel ready, without a plan, a
  budget or anyone's permission, and let the doing teach you. Once you've
  begun, the advice shifts to showing up consistently. (Two halves of the
  corpus were analyzed separately; they produced the same top two in the same
  order.)

   1. Just start — action beats preparation .......................... 71
   2. Show up consistently; discipline over inspiration .............. 51
   3. Forge your own path — don't imitate ............................ 38
   4. Find your people and community ................................. 36
   5. Make peace with failure, rejection and slow timelines .......... 33
   6. Trust your instincts over outside opinion ...................... 27
   7. Give yourself permission — no one else's is needed ............. 24
   8. Do it for love, not approval ................................... 21
   9. Support yourself financially while you build ................... 20
      Also: pay close attention to the world (16), start small with what you
      have (15), learn the business side (14), study the craft and the people
      you admire (13).

    · Joe Hagan: "There's sort of a psychological moment where you have to
      bite the bullet and just start."
    · Alabaster dePlume: "Do it now before we are ready. Do it even if we're
      telling you not to."
    · Andrew Aydin: "I think the first rule, if you want to make comics, is to
      make comics."
    · Louise Campion: "You can't count on inspiration, you have to count on
      discipline"
    · Trixie Mattel: "My best advice is, don't take anybody's advice."

COLLABORATING — 217 people gave advice on this
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Clearly: pick the right people — for shared sensibility, complementary
  skills and trust, more than raw talent. It's cited more than twice as often
  as anything else. After that, the advice is about openness: welcome
  surprise, then trust collaborators and let go.

   1. Pick the right people .......................................... 62
   2. Welcome the unexpected — differences are the material .......... 29
   3. Trust them and let go of control ............................... 24
   4. Talk it out — be honest, address conflict, write it down ....... 18
   5. Build the scaffolding — roles, schedules, contracts ............ 18
   6. Learn from each other .......................................... 16
   7. Meet in the middle — accountability and give-and-take .......... 15
   8. Hold your own center ........................................... 14
   9. Give back generously ........................................... 13
  10. Check your ego ................................................. 12
      Also: know when to say no or let it die (11), build trust over time
      (11).

    · Kim Gordon: "I like playing with Bill, and that has a lot to do with the
      fact that we have a shared sensibility."
    · merritt K: "I don't want to be working with someone who is too much like
      me. I'm already enough of me for myself."
    · Carla Perez-Gallardo: "saying 'yes, and…' is a really healthy framework
      for collaboration."
    · Roger O'Donnell: "You have to trust them and respect them enough to let
      them put their own stamp on it."
    · Christian Lee Hutson: "the most important part of being collaborative is
      being willing to sound stupid and have bad ideas."

4. PEOPLE BY PRACTICE
==============================================================================


HARDCORE VOCALISTS — 9 (+15 borderline)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Rule: Vocalist of a hardcore punk band (incl. post-hardcore, metalcore).
  Relies partly on outside knowledge of the bands; "borderline" = hardcore
  roots or an adjacent genre. (Alexis Marshall of Daughters would count, but
  his interview is unpublished.)
  - Eugene Robinson  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-and-vocalist-eugene-robinson-on-performance-chasing-perfection-and-resisting-the-need-to-explain-your-work/
      "a video of when Whipping Boy played with Minor Threat in Lansing, Michigan in 1981"
  - Geoff Rickly  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/writer-and-musician-geoff-rickly-on-the-benefits-of-an-interdisciplinary-practice/
      "roster: writer, musician — Thursday (post-hardcore) vocalist; "saw the Bad Brains and overnight switched into hardcore""
  - Greg Puciato  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-greg-puciato-on-always-keeping-yourself-open-to-new-ideas/
      "roster: Musician — The Dillinger Escape Plan vocalist"
  - Henry Rollins  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/henry-rollins-on-defining-success/
      "roster: Jack-of-all-trades — Black Flag vocalist"
  - Ian MacKaye  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/ian-mackaye-and-brandon-stosuy-on-independence-creativity-and-the-creative-independent/
      "roster: Musician, Label Owner — Minor Threat, Fugazi vocalist"
  - Jacob Bannon  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-and-artist-jacob-bannon-on-multitasking-craftsmanship-and-letting-go-of-your-work/
      "roster: Musician, Visual artist — Converge vocalist"
  - Jeremy Bolm  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-jeremy-bolm-on-facing-life-through-your-art/
      "Touché Amoré vocalist — "working on Touché stuff"; "there wasn't really any other hardcore records"  [added in manual re"
  - Justin Pearson  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-justin-pearson-on-making-art-for-yourself/
      "My first band, Struggle, played at a campsite where they had all-you-can-eat spaghetti"
  - Mary Jane Dunphe  [yes]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-mary-jane-dunphe-on-making-art-that-grounds-you/
      "Vexx (Olympia hardcore punk) vocalist — "my old punk band, Vexx"  [added in manual review]"
  Borderline:
  - Aaron Turner  [borderline]  https://thecreativeindependent.com/people/musician-and-visual-artist-aaron-turner-on-finding-strength-in-limitations/
      "roster: musician and artist — Isis/Old Man Gloom vocalist (post-metal, hardcore-rooted)"
